ParlaMint-AT_2010-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00074_d8e9022	Thank you, Madam President. Estimated Minister of State! Appreciate High House! This was another example of what politics would look like if Mr. Kunasek were to rule: A deportation order for the bad air, the fine dust abroad, and the story is done! – If it were so easy! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, we all like living in fresh and good air. Austria is a tourist country – we even sell it that way. We know that good air is a health factor and that the fine dust load, the nitrogen oxide load is a topic for the health of adults, but especially the children. It's been known for a long time. In Austria, national and national measurements have been carried out since 1998 under the responsibility of the national leaders. We have only recently discussed the IG-Air Report 2006-2008, and we see that measures are possible, but good measures work better. Over the last few years, national leaders have repeatedly called for greater coordination between other and new measures. We all have experienced that good measures have good effects. We took away the acid rain, we took the sulphur away. We managed to get rid of the FC cars. Nothing broke out. All the people used to it. It's just not a problem. It is therefore not necessary to play up the problem of the IG air, but we simply have to take care of what is happening to us. So far, we have the problem that we are not sufficiently involved in air pollution values in critical times, because we have too few instruments and have to intervene far too intensively with the existing instruments, so that individual groups would be severely affected in their economic activity, in their habit of life. The IG air, which we are presenting today, will allow much more action, will also allow a smooth and smooth transition into all areas of the economy, of daily life, so that there may be serious interventions at all only on very few days a year, or we will not need them if the measures are effective. The most important thing is that we decide today with this law something that allows for action in a wide range and therefore the depth of the individual measures, which reduces the intensity of the intervention. In good German: If everyone does something, you don't have to hurt anyone. – This is actually the principle of this law. Of course, it means a change for all, and it must be taken into account that there is a good division of work between the federal government and countries. Countries are responsible for many areas. We can't apply anything to them. The fire of the house, for example, is an issue that the countries themselves have to regulate. If Graz has major problems because the geographical situation is such that in certain weather conditions the air remains from Graz in Graz and then becomes a problem, then you will probably only be able to solve the problem in Graz. There are new instruments with this law, but the contribution of the house fire to the burdened air in Graz will only be regulated again by national law. The whole effect will only be achieved in Graz if measures similar to those in Vienna are put in place. In Vienna, for example, it has succeeded in bringing the fuel oil out of practical use. Heating oil is bad: for trade balance, for climate change and for air pollution. In Vienna, it has succeeded in significantly reducing the use of heating oil and thus also the burden by changing to district heating, including by switching to natural gas. That is really the point: away from oil. But that's still going to be the subject today. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Mein Ladies and gentlemen, the heads of state are actively involved in the practical implementation of this law and are intensely required to measure, analyse, identify where measures are necessary, then wisely choose the right measures from the possible mix of measures and to implement them in the right place at the right time with the time limit necessary. That means: at the right time on the spot. I very much believe in subsidiarity, and I also very much believe in federalism. Of course it's clear: Closer to the citizen also means that the citizen is very responsible. I therefore believe that the Heads of State will not use any of these measures longer than is absolutely necessary. General measures with a three-month limit in the transport sector are often only necessary for hours or often only at part-time periods. That must then be said to people. That is why there is now a new regulation for the traffic control systems, i.e. the advertisements we see, what we have to do and what is going on. This is a new opportunity for the countries. In the financing this was an interesting discussion. Ladies and gentlemen, we will not be able to solve all the questions with this law. We all need to get better at each other in the measurement so that we can also take the right measures internationally. Transport is very happy to be presented as the problem. This will create room for new investments in an airspace situation, which are environmentally friendly, which do not affect the environment and are important for our economic performance and economic future. Value creation comes only from the progress and not from the defense of the old instincts, and therefore it is necessary to take measures so that new, good installations can also be put in place and approved. It was quite up-to-date that, in the case of necessary measures for air pollution of the federal government, the competences were given to the countries, but, at the same time, so many restrictions and exceptions were made by law, that it was very difficult for national leaders to adopt and then implement real measures. In Vienna, about 400 construction machines are in use in the annual average. Due to all these exceptions to the law that existed, the Vienna head of state was precisely responsible for seven of them. All the others were excluded. The fact that construction machines are machines that are immensely polluting the air is known. These infinitely many exceptions will no longer exist in the future. What will no longer exist in the future is that these construction machines, these off-road machines, can just smell ahead of themselves and be used so much without any checks. There will also be a regular review for all off-road machines over 18 kilowatts in the future, and one will see how these can develop and prescribe improvements. The environmental zones have been much debated and have been highly criticised in advance. With this law, we only create the technical requirement for vehicles to get different color pickers depending on how much pollutant they emit. This gives the national leaders the opportunity to adopt, on the one hand, environmental zones and, on the other, to categorize which vehicles are allowed to drive in loaded areas and which are not. Well, I think that's quite a bit Meaningful. We will also take into account the desire of some federal states – in particular the Styria with Graz. There we want to introduce so-called environmental zones. De jure is not the environmental zones, it has to be said quite clearly, but rehabilitation areas – in the Volksmund termed environmental zones. What I'm concerned with is the following: If a region says it wants to introduce such things, then it makes sense that we supply the cars to exhaust gas classes with a pickerl, and that should not be a green in Graz, in Vienna a red and in Salzburg a yellow one, but it should be uniformly marked throughout Austria. Whether a region carries out such a rehabilitation area, a restriction on vehicles of certain exhaust gas classes, is to be left to the region. Here, the law provides flexibility to take different measures. I think that's very important. There will also be a simplified coverage of traffic restrictions. The horror propaganda, which has been spread here, that one can only read speed limits in future on the Internet, is absolutely ridiculous. That was mediatically played. It was never planned, but it was clearly identified as areas for renovation. But you know that there have been problems in Graz because there has not been a proper rallying here. And that is why we have had to provide for appropriate regulations in order to make this better in the future. As far as transport, road development and road construction are concerned, it is now the case that not only the emissions in the construction of a new road, but also the expected emissions in future transport on this road will be taken into account in the re-approval process. This is also intended to protect health – above all, people who live there are protected. Finally, it is also a question of taking into account the desire of the countries to provide for an option of an official complaint against criminal offences of the UVS for the head of state. That was a wish of the federal states, which we take into account here. In conclusion: I am expressly grateful to the negotiating team, to the staff of the Ministry of Life, to my house, to many who have contributed to this, especially at the parliamentary level – Mr Schultes, colleague Bayr, who has been very active here in the final phase, that we can complete the law. It has succeeded in bringing a matter of the most difficult kind, with different, divergent interests into a hat: Economy, transport and, above all, the concern of the population to ensure health for the population. I thank everyone and seek to agree.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00074_d8e10129	Next speaker: Mr. Dr. Kurzmann. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00074_d8e10150	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! This law allows countries the possibility of introducing environmental zones by regulation in the future. The so-called environmental zone Graz will probably be the first field of experimentation in Austria, and we as the Steirer, as a Grazer, are convinced that we will have similar bad experiences with the introduction of their environmental zones by the German cities. We liberals therefore reject the introduction of these environmental zones for easily comprehensible, plausible reasons. On the one hand, there are social reasons that prevent us from agreeing to this law: The commuters, ladies and gentlemen, and the majority of drivers, usually cannot afford to give up a four-year-old diesel vehicle and buy a new one for it. For more than 50 percent of the passenger cars – this has already been said by my previous speaker, Mr Kunasek – this would mean in Styria that they can no longer enter Graz's capital city, that Graz would become a kind of restricted zone. This would be a smooth blow for the commuters, because they depend on the motor vehicle to get to their workplace at all. However, thousands of Grazers would also be directly affected by such a measure, and in a short time or in the middle of a severe economic crisis. Ladies and gentlemen, there are also economic reasons that lead us to the meeting for our convictions. An environmental zone Graz would be another heavy blow against the economic location of Styria. Trade in the city center of Graz would be heavily affected, another loss of purchasing power would be the logical consequence. That is why the Chamber of Commerce in Styria made a clear stand against it months ago. In Graz and Graz alone, the car owners would suffer a loss in value of their vehicles of about €70 million. The third and equally important reason is the lack of cost-benefit compensation, ladies and gentlemen. Environmental zones in German cities have shown one thing very clearly: For example, the fine dust pollution in Stuttgart or Bremen has hardly declined after the introduction of these environmental zones. However, if one calculates the administrative costs that have exploded – for posters, for monitoring – then that alone will result in an additional cost of €70 000 for Bremen. We are convinced that it is more sensible to further reduce the house fire – the main cause of fine dust – by targeted measures. We do not need bans in our cities that further restrict mobility that is urgently needed at this time. We also reject alibi measures that burden workers, cause high administrative costs, and ultimately do nothing to the environment. How much this issue moves people in our country, ladies and gentlemen, shows the result of a signature action that we set up and carried out in Styria a few months ago. Within a short time, we have combined more than 10,000 signatures, and 10,000 people in our country have signed a petition against the Graz environmental zone. This clearly shows how the population thinks. This shows who represents the interests of the commuters – the Freedom Party – who represents the interests of the less-favoured in this country, who cannot constantly afford a new motor vehicle, who represents the small traders, the small economic operators, and who, last but not least, represents the drivers who must once again retain as a milk cow of the nation. [[Abg. Dr. Stumbling: The ÖVP!]] [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] Therefore, we will not be able to give our consent to this bill.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00074_d8e10214	Ladies and gentlemen! There has been an analysis by the Ministry of the Environment in order to be able to respond accordingly. What now are the essential, important innovations in the IG air to properly implement the European Air Quality Directive and to achieve our national environmental objectives more quickly? With regard to the implementation of the Air Quality Directive, the fine dust loads from the winter scatter will be calculated in future, but at the same time a new limit for the PM2.5 fine dust class will be introduced. This creates new, very ambitious obligations for this limit value. From our point of view, this has already been mentioned, a very important change is that the possibility will be given to the national leaders – because the IG air is only the framework, the main part is in the field of action of the countries – to put in place measures in the field of target and source traffic, that concrete measures can be put against too high air loads. Examples have already been mentioned for this purpose. This law will only be in line with the efficiency expectations if particularly restrictive measures are taken in the federal states. The novelles will also significantly reduce the fine dust load. Especially when travelling up to 5 kilometers, that is within a short distance, this is a good way, and you are also the fastest on the road. In this context, I would just like to mention that bicycle traffic in Vienna is currently 5 per cent, and the aim is to increase the share to 8 per cent by 2015. This, of course, also requires further expansion of the cycle paths. The network now covers 1,200 kilometres. Today, 25 kilometers will be added again. What impressed me in this context is the free wheel system: In the previous year alone, there were 400 000 city bike rides, with over 1.3 million kilometers of travel – that is, a considerable contribution. The exception catalogue was criticized in § 14. But that's very difficult. What would you take if you said that's too extensive? . I say, for example, that the exception that farmers and forestrymen may use partly old vehicles in their main activity is understandable to me. That can be the same from my point of view. In any case, we will continue to reduce transport emissions and create incentives with various measures to bring structurally outdated and climate-politically troubling plants to the state of the art. In summary, this innovation represents a very substantial progress towards the legal situation so far.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00074_d8e13143	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! In fact, what has happened and is happening in the Gulf of Mexico for weeks is an ecological drama. In reality, the oil industry is experiencing a super-GAU in the truest sense of the word, the Chernobyl of the oil industry, with inevitable consequences to the coming years and decades. It is absolutely disastrous that all the rescue tests do not have an effect or only show partial effect. However, the discussion also results in something good, because now reports and reports are suddenly emerging worldwide, which show how problematic such drilling is, with what high risk they are being discharged and what is still in the room. It is therefore good that we take advantage of this opportunity to have an absolute, fundamental debate. Yes, I say we have to get out of the fossil oil industry because it's not a perspective – not just because that happened in the Gulf of Mexico, but because the fossil oil reserves are going to end anyway and because I'm of the opinion and the deep conviction: The earlier a region, a state diversified its energy system, converted its energy system and put it on new legs, the earlier it has the nose and this will be better for the society of this region, that state. That is why I am quite clear that we are pushing this exit and moving forward. This brings Austria something, namely environmental quality, regional value added and also green jobs, because we thus guarantee prospects. Therefore – I have repeatedly explained this, and I also stick to my topic – I am an energy-autarchic Austria, I think this is a fascinating vision. I know we're gonna need fossil oil and gas, but a forced exit makes sense. All the other states do the same. The United States, for example, says that they also want to become energy self-sufficient, so they are drilling in their territory to become independent of energy supplies from other regions. This is also true in Austria in the sense of the transfer. We also want to become independent of regions which are partly crisis regions that were once militaryly strong and now use energy as a political means of printing. That's why you have to get out there and prepare for this exit. Of course, the energy autarchy is not a thing that goes from today to tomorrow, with a finger snap, that's probably clear to everyone, but it's about us consistently moving this forward. We have done so with the “Energiestrategy Österreich” because for the first time, there is a plan to invest gradually in renewable energy while at the same time achieving climate targets. This has not yet been done. Of course, it is true that this plan, this energy strategy, must also be met with life, as is already the case in the approach. For example, the support campaign for photovoltaics – €35 million, we have already talked about it today – should bring a boost towards photovoltaics, but at the same time also put the industry in the position that these panels, the technical buildings, become more competitive and economical. On the side of my ministry something is also done, I also do something to achieve the energy autarchy. We started last year the program for the climate and energy model regions because there are many cities and communities in Austria that say they want to become, like Güssing, the first energy-autarchic city of Austria. We have had a call for proposals for this programme to the 50 regions; 37 regions have met the strict criteria and have been working on energy autarchy for their region since the previous year. These regions are distributed throughout Austria, giving a good example of how it works. There are still enough people running around in Austria who say that energy autarchy is not only impossible to think, but also practically impossible to achieve. These regions are trying to prove it and are therefore important partners for me. [[Abg. May. Brunner: But they need...!]] I will continue this programme this year to motivate new regions to go this way because it is indisputably necessary that we save energy, use energy efficiently and, of course, also generate renewable energy. Domestic environmental promotion is an important contribution to this. Where the economy, the whole tourist sector is in the situation of saving energy, is encouraged by the state to invest in more energy efficiency. This extends to the climate protection programme “climate:aktiv”, where I have more than 800 partners in Austria, cities, communities, companies, companies that are involved here, for example in public transport, at ÖBB and Postbus, which make their drivers, their chauffeurs spritspar trainings and thus save themselves a large amount of fuel. These are very small initiatives, but they are necessary because we need such initiatives throughout Austria. Many companies, for example, are already handling their fleet and are moving more towards alternative means of drive such as bike traffic and are forcing efficient fleet management. Yes, an ecologization of the tax system also makes sense! It must be true that the one who behaves environmentally friendlyly is rewarded and the one who behaves environmentally harmfully. Ecologisation of the tax system is not simply the introduction of any energy tax or the increase of a tax, but it clearly needs an overall approach. [[Abg. May. Kogler: Exactly!]] This must lead the press to raise money on the one hand, but on the other hand there is a social balance, for example a compensation for commuters or for energy-intensive industries. [[Abg. May. Brunner: When do you do it?]] That must be an overall concept, but a real one. In any case, I'm going to work for it because it makes sense. In the same way as transport: We need transport; as Minister of the Environment, I am concerned that he will be environmentally friendly. Therefore, it is an important part of the energy strategy that we rely more on alternative fuels, on bio-sprit, to alternative forms of drive, as it represents electromobility. I'm driving an electric car as a service car to see that it's okay, and also to prove that it's okay. I started the climate tour through Austria, in Vienna and in all of the country's capitals, to present alternative drive systems – not only in the area of passenger cars, but also e-bikes, e-scooters, alternative drive systems; people are interested in this too! – to present them: Yes, it works! You can convince yourself that this climate tour works very well. Further, there is also an outflow towards space heat, towards thermal renovation as part of the energy strategy. In concrete terms, a result of this oil crisis in the Gulf of Mexico was my proposal – the more concrete proposal – the introduction of an eco-fund, in which oil companies pay down and thus feed on a pot from which any environmental damage is also financed, otherwise the public will have to bear. They experience it especially in the United States, where there are miserable processes of liability issues over years, over decades; the affected population has nothing of it. It is therefore necessary that those who internalize profits do not externalize costs and raise public awareness of them, but that those who benefit from them also help to prevent such environmental damage, but also to force us to move towards renewable energy. We need money to do that, the state does not have so much funding, since it is not so devious and quite right that the energy industry itself finances it. It should also be able to benefit from diversification and investment in new energy systems. I will present this idea to the Council of Environment Ministers in Brussels next week in order to make progress on the subject in this body, for example, to support Commissioner Oettinger in his efforts – in his not easy efforts. This was discussed, you can imagine: They are mega-interests behind them, very many financial interests, very many job interests. This is a very, very tough system that needs to be changed, but at some point you have to start with it. We are there – I am definitely on my way to do that. If I find a partner here with you, I am glad because it is necessary that we can convince people in a movement and thus also change big things. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00074_d8e14040	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen in the House! Dear visitors! Today we decide here the Medical Law, the Medical GmbH. This is a long-term demand of the doctors, which is now being realized, it is a long-standing demand of the Freedom Party, and it is above all a long-standing demand of the patients, because what does this Doctors GmbH mean? – It essentially means restructuring of outpatient care for patients. It will be so that patients may also be able to reach their personal doctor or medical practice in the early evening, evening and weekend in the outpatient area outside the hospitals. This is a milestone in medical care, and I believe, Minister, you can be proud that it falls within your term of office that you now hold this office. It is of course true that much of what is contained in this law could be improved. I just want to say a few things. For example, it is true that the Board of Commerce highly criticises the restriction on five persons per shareholder outside the professional groups. She said that this was an insufficient demarcation to the normal ambulatory supply, and this could perhaps constitute an arbitrary definition and a non-constitutional one. - We'll see if that's true. A second point, which I personally do not like at all, is to include constituency practice with reimbursement of costs in the need assessment of the establishment plans. This is also something which could possibly be unconstitutional and thus lead to a constitutional complaint and thus to a repeal of the law. Finally, the issue of the last few days, which has really caused a lot of dust: liability insurance for all doctors, ambulances and private hospitals. There was indeed a provision in the draft which, apart from the current provision, would have meant an incalculable reinsurance risk for the reinsurers and, of course, a massive cost increase. Until now, the doctors have been freely insured. With free insurance I mean voluntary insurance. For example, I am a dentist, I think, with 2 million € versi heret, and I am available this insurance sum three times a year – that is, of course, illusory. Until now, this condition is just a good one and has actually worked perfectly. In addition, it is the case that the medical chambers have set up conciliatory bodies that are relatively uncomplicated in trying to achieve compensation for damages when patients are harmed, which is also assumed in about 60 percent of cases. The draft, which is available, would have provided that no insurance limit would have existed. This would have meant that a ceiling of €3 million could have been unreflected and actually unlimited, which would have represented an incalculable risk, with the fear of our side and also of the insurance association that the insurance companies would have been expensive by about €50 million, but not only the insurances, but of course also the health system, and finally the patients would have stayed on it. That is why we are glad that we have made a five-party request that would undermine these provisions. In the future, there will be compulsory professional liability insurance. However, this is limited to the amount of € 2 million three times a year for professional doctors and five times a year for ambulatories – or, as they are so beautifully called, the GmbHs. That's what we can live with. We can also live by the fact that there is a patient's direct right of access to insurance. In other words, the patient no longer has to reconcile with the doctor if he wants to sue the cause of the damage concerned, but he directs himself to the insurance as is common in the car industry. However, it must not be forgotten that in the car industry, i.e. in the car insurance sector, 5 million people are insured, de facto 5 million cars, the doctors are about a group of 18 000, which, of course, is not in proportion; therefore, this should not have been argued. In other words, we are glad that, with our resistance and our request, which we have already made in committee, where it has been voted down by all political groups, we have brought about a change of attitude, not only for the doctors but especially for the patients, because they do not have to fear that there will be an incalculable increase in their patient accounts.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00074_d8e15617	Mr President, I should like to thank the President-in-Office of Federal Minister! Appreciate High House! The amendment of the Pharmacy Act corresponds to a concern of the Pharmacy Chamber. As already mentioned, the possibility of withdrawing confidence from the President and Vice-President of a regional office is now being created. It also regulates the establishment of the number of mandates of the departmental committee, the departmental assembly. In the interests of a uniform procedure in the two institutions, the Chamber Assembly and the Delegate Assembly, and a fair distribution, the number of members of the Länder in the respective departments will now serve as a basis for determining the remaining mandates in the Department Committee. This change also entails a new beginning of this term of office. The term of office is 1st. ==References====External links== ==References== This shift can now better accommodate and coordinate the deadlines and dates in the electoral procedures. With this new law, an editorial report will also be removed. With regard to Mr Spadiut's request, where the change in voting mode is required, I would like to say that there is no need on the part of the Chamber of Pharmacy and that we will not vote in the same way as in the Committee. In summary, this change of law entails harmonisation of the definition of the number of mandates, that organisational concerns are taken into account, that this proposal includes important democratic mechanisms and that the amendment to the Pharmacy Chamber Act has been brought about with the greatest participation and in full agreement with the Office, the Pharmacy Chamber. I would like to thank all those who have contributed to this agreement and the Committee's unanimity confirms good cooperation. Thank you.

It might be that treatments abroad are better. However, a number of patients have already suffered serious health problems after poor quality treatment – some have even died. Maybe foreign doctors are more friendly? If you go in so many ambulances, you might think that this accusation might apply. The question now arises why it is cheaper there. We have often discussed this too: Missing social standards, lower wages and, above all, cheaper purchases make these prices possible. The paradoxical situation, however, that Austrian health insurance companies then pay the costs incurred abroad upon presentation of the invoice, which means that the Austrian The Chinese economy is creating massive purchasing power outflows and, as a result of a really high loss of domestic jobs, only rounds off the negative picture. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] However, we are unfortunately moving in a grey area. In my opinion, the health insurance companies are relatively happy that social outsourcing is being carried out in certain areas, so that social responsibility is also transferred to neighbouring countries. Through this outsourcing of social responsibility to neighbouring countries, especially in the field of dentistry, Austria is missing a double-digit million annually, which is then missing for the financing of our solidar system. This week we discussed quite a lot about foreign social transfers too. Apart from that, it is highly unsocial to exclude most of our population from treatments that are not manageable in Austria. I therefore urge you to submit a study on the outflow of social security funds from Austrian insured persons abroad and its impact over the last ten years. Furthermore, there is a need to raise the resulting loss of purchasing power and the related loss of domestic jobs. That's why we want to be able to counter-tax. I mean, a reasonable answer to this would be to create facilities in Austria – across all the social partners, along with the Ministry – in order to give our people here in Austria a really social-friendly treatment.

[[BZÖ's condolences.]] That is the central point, because the decree is not binding. That is to say, no citizen can appeal to this decree from the outside, but it is only a relationship between the issuing body, in the case of the Federal Minister of Justice, and the implementing body in question, in most cases the appropriate judicial body. I propose another amendment which has the following content – I now believe that it is a command of civil kindness –: In Austria, we still have the situation that, due to the rules in force in the criminal procedure, you, Mr Amon, can make a statement, you can also sign it, but you do not get a copy of what you have said yourself, because You have no legal right to do so. ParlaMint-AT_2010-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00074_d8e25275	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Under this agenda there are some changes to the trade regulations, but also to the IG-air, and follow-up in the field of trade regulations to the discussion. That's just a formality, but it's a certain difference. As far as the trade regulations and the essential contents that are being changed are concerned, we are also producing protection for the self-employed in the construction site directive. With regard to professional access to the smoking industry, we are reorganising the removal of the requirement of Austrian citizenship accordingly. With regard to the host garden scheme just mentioned, I would like to inform you that this is a certain balancing act between the rights of the neighbours and the right of establishment, the business authorisation procedure. We have considered this very carefully and have come to the decision, that is to say, with regard to the practice of the Constitutional Court of Justice, to try to standardize what the Constitutional Court of Justice has taken into account in its decisions. In essence, what we have received with ‘rest’, with ‘grill ban’ and the like, is in the sense of a standard regulation, nothing other than an attempt to to have a typical definition of host gardens where the average view indicates that the neighbours are not affected. That means in the clear text: We have had the problem that the trader often took a very long time until he got the approval at all. Now we've turned it over; it's reported, it's approved, and the authority has to represent the corresponding violations and can cause the closure on its own, if there are violations. This is in the final effect a much better procedure, which will allow the respective host gardens with up to 75 places to obtain the approval faster. As far as we have seen this in the industry, this is also quite acceptable. I don't expect us to have any neighboring impairments. We will see what steps will be taken in this direction. It is sometimes the case that there is something different about verifications. But we are relatively sure that we have taken everything into account. This is essentially to be said on these points: Formalisation requirements on the part of the EU and a somewhat more practical implementation on the part of the host gardens. - Thank you.
